<a id="5007-noface3d-noise-scheduled-focused-anchoring-and-controlled-enhancement-for-3d-generation"></a>

### 5007\. [NOFACE3D: NOise-scheduled Focused Anchoring and Controlled Enhancement for 3D Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13050)
**ArXiv:** 2610.13050 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13050)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13050.pdf)]

**Authors:** James Rose, Jingwen Liang, Wenchao Ma, Oren Jacob, Haomiao Jiang

**Abstract:** Recent image-to-3D models generate complete, high-quality assets from a single image, with global shape and appearance that are already convincing; what remains is the fine scale, where small, thin, elongated, or detail-rich regions are reconstructed softly and under-resolved. This gap can arise in generators with fixed-resolution spatial grids: the same spatial sampling density is used for a large, smooth, low-curvature surface and for a thin, high-curvature structure that carries most of the perceived detail. To this end, we introduce NOFACE3D, a training-free framework that refines these detail-critical regions while preserving the rest of the asset. Given a generated mesh, its input image, and a refinement box, NOFACE3D extracts the selected submesh, scales it up to fill the generator's input volume, and re-encodes it into the full-resolution latent grid. Conditioned on the image crop, it refines the region through flow-based sampling with a spatially varying noise schedule that anchors boundary latents. The refined region is then integrated back into the original asset, preserving the surrounding geometry and appearance. We implement NOFACE3D on TRELLIS.2 and its sparse voxel representation; all of it operates purely at inference time on the frozen pretrained generator. We evaluate NOFACE3D on PAniC-3D, TexVerse and BigDetail, a new benchmark of 339 assets with annotated detail-critical regions. On all three, it generates sharper geometry and finer texture detail than the baselines. Project page: https://idkcouscous.github.io/NoFACE3D/

**Comment:** Criterion 5: NOFACE3D applies training-free, spatially noise-scheduled flow refinement to frozen TRELLIS.2, producing sharper geometry and finer textures on PAniC-3D, TexVerse, and the new 339-asset BigDetail benchmark.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5036-channel-match-calibrating-shape-latents-for-few-step-3d-generation"></a>

### 5036\. [Channel Match: Calibrating Shape Latents for Few-Step 3D Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15321)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15321 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15321)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15321.pdf)]

**Authors:** Di Yang, Zhihao Li, Yanhai Xiong, Yufei Wang

**Abstract:** Recent native 3D generators achieve high-quality asset creation by modeling structured 3D latents with flow-based transformers, but their high-resolution generation remains computationally expensive due to costly iterative sampling. In this work, we introduce a few-step distillation framework for native 3D generation, enabled by continuous self-consistency training with a custom JVP FlashAttention kernel and an analytic velocity regression objective for sparse structured 3D latents. To stabilize joint optimization, we further introduce an adaptive loss weighting strategy that automatically balances them without manual tuning. Beyond acceleration, we reveal a previously overlooked failure mode of few-step native 3D generation: sampling-induced latent distribution drift preserves geometric correctness but disrupts downstream PBR texture synthesis, causing texture artifacts on geometrically valid assets. To address this issue, we propose Channel Match, a training-free adaptive per-channel latent calibration method that restores the latent distribution without modifying either generator. We validate our approach on TRELLIS.2 at the $1024^3$ resolution setting. Our two-step model achieves $4.5\times$ faster geometry sampling while maintaining geometry quality comparable to the many-step teacher, and Channel Match further improves rendering quality by recovering the latent interface between geometry and appearance generation.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Two-step distillation accelerates TRELLIS.2 geometry sampling by 4.5× at 1024³ resolution while maintaining comparable geometry quality, and training-free Channel Match latent calibration reduces downstream PBR texture artifacts.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5041-uniforge3d-joint-3d-reconstruction-and-generation-in-a-shared-anchor-guided-space"></a>

### 5041\. [UniForge3D: Joint 3D Reconstruction and Generation in a Shared Anchor-Guided Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15357)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15357 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15357)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15357.pdf)]

**Authors:** Wenjue Chen, Wenyue Chen, Heliang Zheng, ..., Yuan Liu, Meng Li

**Abstract:** Generating complete 3D assets from sparse, unposed images requires preserving observed geometry while completing unseen regions. Joint reconstruction and generation offers a way to address both goals: reconstruction recovers the geometry observed in the input images, while generation contributes learned shape priors. However, combining camera-relative reconstruction with canonical-space generation introduces a coordinate mismatch that makes direct geometric interaction more difficult. Moreover, conditioning each 3D location on all views without accounting for their relevance may mix useful geometric evidence with unrelated image content, hindering local detail recovery. We present UniForge3D, which jointly models reconstruction and generation in a shared anchor-guided space, resolving their coordinate mismatch. This space follows the anchor view's azimuth while keeping the object upright. The two branches exchange features bidirectionally to predict per-view point maps and a complete sparse voxel structure. We further condition point-map prediction on the generated structure to establish pixel-to-voxel correspondences and select the most informative input view for each voxel. Features from the selected views then guide detailed shape generation, helping preserve observed geometric details. Our method achieves state-of-the-art appearance and geometry results on Toys4K and GSO, generating complete 3D assets with improved fidelity. Project webpage: https://xishuxishu.github.io/uniforge3d-project/

**Comment:** Criteria 5 and 6: UniForge3D jointly reconstructs and generates complete 3D assets from sparse, unposed images through a shared anchor-guided space and bidirectional reconstruction–generation feature exchange, achieving state-of-the-art geometry and appearance on Toys4K and GSO.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5082-worldrecon-generative-multi-view-mesh-reconstruction-for-large-scale-3d-world"></a>

### 5082\. [WorldRecon: Generative Multi-View Mesh Reconstruction for Large-Scale 3D World](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18846)
**ArXiv:** 2610.18846 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18846)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18846.pdf)]

**Authors:** Mengchen Zhang, Yang Li, Chunshi Wang, Tong Wu, Chunchao Guo

**Abstract:** Reconstructing complete, high-quality meshes of large-scale scenes from unposed multi-view images requires faithful geometric grounding while plausibly completing surfaces that are unobserved or only sparsely captured. We present WorldRecon, a generative multi-view reconstruction framework for recovering complete, large-scale 3D scene meshes from unposed RGB images. WorldRecon uses a coarse, noisy global point cloud and camera poses estimated from the input images by a reconstruction model as a global scaffold for whole-scene generation and partitions it into overlapping spatial blocks. To robustly leverage these imperfect geometric priors, we equip the native 3D generative model TRELLIS.2 with a dedicated reconstruction-based conditioning architecture that generates each block from its local point-cloud crop and block-relevant image observations, producing sparse structure, detailed geometry, and PBR materials. To maintain scene-level coherence, we introduce neighbour-aware latent inpainting and a causal world-generation procedure that propagates structural, geometric, and material context across overlapping blocks. Together, these designs scale multi-view generative reconstruction to large-scale scenes, yielding coherent, unified meshes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that WorldRecon achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction performance, reducing Chamfer distance by 14.9% and 43.1% while improving F-score by 17.3% and 12.0% over the strongest baselines on synthetic and real-world indoor scenes, respectively, thereby demonstrating faithful and complete generative reconstruction as well as strong sim-to-real generalization. Project page: https://kszpxxzmc.github.io/WorldRecon-website/.

**Comment:** Criteria 5 and 6: WorldRecon conditions TRELLIS.2 on geometry estimated from unposed multi-view images and introduces neighbour-aware latent inpainting for coherent large-scale mesh generation, reducing Chamfer distance by 14.9% and 43.1% on synthetic and real-world indoor scenes, respectively.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5102-q-magic-quad-only-mesh-autoregressive-generation-via-singularity-aware-circular-relative-offset-tokenization"></a>

### 5102\. [Q-MAGIC: Quad-only Mesh Autoregressive Generation via Singularity-Aware Circular Relative Offset Tokenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19777)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19777 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19777)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19777.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yuming Zhao, Zezeng Li, Weimin Wang, Xiang Wu, Na Lei

**Abstract:** Autoregressive models have proven effective for triangular mesh generation by transforming it into predicting the next face, converting the non-differentiable connectivity prediction into a sequence of token predictions. However, generating quad-only meshes remains challenging because quad faces impose stricter topological constraints than triangles. The model must not only serialize quad faces into a valid token sequence, but also support unambiguous connectivity reconstruction during de-serialization. To address this, we propose Q-MAGIC, a novel autoregressive model for quad mesh generation. We first introduce a tailored serialization scheme for quad meshes, which requires at least two new vertices for the next face and is less flexible than triangles due to strict wiring constraints. To meet these requirements and reduce serialization length, we propose a singularity-aware serialization that prioritizes wiring from singular points, leading to more coherent traversals. Additionally, we propose a circular relative offset tokenization that encodes geometry mainly via local relative offsets. Built upon this representation, an autoregressive Transformer learns the underlying distribution of quad meshes via next-token prediction, enabling effective quad mesh generation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Q-MAGIC produces more compact sequences while consistently improving geometric fidelity and mesh quality compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Comment:** Criteria 5 and 6: Q-MAGIC introduces autoregressive quad-only mesh generation using singularity-aware serialization and circular relative offset tokenization, producing more compact sequences and improving geometric fidelity and mesh quality over state-of-the-art methods.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5111-towards-physically-grounded-articulated-3d-assets-a-multi-agent-pipeline-and-benchmark"></a>

### 5111\. [Towards Physically Grounded Articulated 3D Assets: A Multi-Agent Pipeline and Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13947)
**ArXiv:** 2610.13947 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13947)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13947.pdf)]

**Authors:** Xudong Cai, Ruijie Zhu, Haoliang Shang, ..., Andrea Vedaldi, Chuanxia Zheng

**Abstract:** In this paper, we study physically grounded articulated 3D asset generation---moving the goal from reproducing how objects look to capturing how they move and function. Inspired by the literature on procedural and agentic generation, we present Artisan, a system that extends the latest method Articraft by introducing a multi-agent pipeline with physical simulation feedback, enabling comprehensive attributes beyond geometry, appearance, and physically plausible articulation, such as collision-free motion, stability under gravity, and accurate mass and material properties. Furthermore, we construct a unified benchmark spanning four key aspects: spatial fidelity, physical plausibility, downstream task performance, and overall quality. The benchmark encompasses 100 reference assets from ArtVIP and LightWheel, 50 stability prompts, and 50 ABO objects with reference masses, with protocols evaluating various metrics. Through extensive evaluation of more than 10 representative models, Artisan achieves competitive spatial fidelity while attaining the lowest collision rates and highest stability rates among the evaluated methods. Together, our system and benchmark establish insights for advancing 3D generation towards physically plausible, functionally usable assets.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Artisan introduces multi-agent articulated 3D asset generation with physical-simulation feedback and a unified quality benchmark, attaining the lowest collision rates and highest stability rates among more than 10 evaluated models.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5115-demiurge-forging-navigable-4d-worlds-via-autoregressive-video-rerendering"></a>

### 5115\. [Demiurge: Forging Navigable 4D Worlds via Autoregressive Video Rerendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14685)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14685 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14685)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14685.pdf)]

**Authors:** Hanxin Zhu, Jianqi Ma, Cong Wang, ..., Zhibo Chen, Jun Zhang

**Abstract:** Recent advances in video diffusion enable novel-view video synthesis from a single input video, supporting world modeling, embodied intelligence, and robotic simulation. However, existing methods process camera trajectories independently, lacking the cross-trajectory consistency needed for coherent, navigable 4D worlds. We introduce Demiurge, an autoregressive video rerendering pipeline built on a dynamic 4D point-cloud memory cache. It promotes cross-trajectory consistency through four components: (1) an appearance video diffusion model that synthesizes photorealistic novel-view videos conditioned on the 4D memory; (2) a memory-consistent video depth completion model that aligns known-region depths with cached geometry and completes missing regions, mitigating scale ambiguity and discontinuities during memory updates; (3) memory-aware dual-stream warping that provides high-fidelity visual guidance and occlusion cues for novel-view synthesis; and (4) geometry-aware video refinement that enhances perceptual quality. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance and three key capabilities: coherent spatiotemporal lattice construction over dense multi-view trajectories; large-parallax video rerendering under challenging camera motions, including 180-degree rotations with no input-view overlap; and geometry-faithful outputs that can be directly optimized into high-quality 3D Gaussian Splatting representations with minimal artifacts. Project page: https://hanxinzhu-lab.github.io/demiurge_project_page/.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Demiurge combines an appearance video diffusion model with dynamic 4D point-cloud memory to generate cross-trajectory-consistent, navigable 4D worlds whose geometry-faithful outputs can be optimized into 3D Gaussian Splatting representations.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5120-umas3d-unified-mask-free-3d-editing-with-region-adaptive-source-preservation"></a>

### 5120\. [UMAS3D: Unified Mask-Free 3D Editing with Region-Adaptive Source Preservation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15533)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15533 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15533)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15533.pdf)]

**Authors:** Xianlin Zeng, Xiaolong Shen, Ruijie Quan, Yi Yang

**Abstract:** 3D editing aims to modify an existing 3D asset according to user intent while preserving geometry and appearance outside the intended edit region. Existing methods often rely on task-specific training, source-3D inversion, or external 3D masks, and lack a unified framework for diverse editing conditions and tasks. We propose UMAS3D, a unified 3D editing framework that is training-free, inversion-free, and free from external 3D masks. UMAS3D converts text, bounding boxes, reference images, and their combinations into common target-view guidance, after which addition, deletion, modification, appearance, and pose editing share the same downstream 3D generation process. Our key insight is to couple online edit-region estimation with region-aware source preservation. Mask-Free Adaptive Region Modeling (MARM) initializes an Edit/Keep prior from source-target visual differences and progressively refines it as the target structure evolves, while Source-Guided Preservation Steering (SGPS) constrains the current Keep-region states to retain source geometry and appearance without suppressing target-driven changes. Experiments show that UMAS3D supports addition, deletion, modification, appearance, and pose editing while effectively preserving unedited source content. These advances make UMAS3D a unified framework for flexible and source-preserving 3D editing across diverse editing conditions.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: UMAS3D advances generative 3D editing through Mask-Free Adaptive Region Modeling and Source-Guided Preservation Steering, enabling training-free, inversion-free editing across multiple input conditions while preserving unedited geometry and appearance.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5122-rethinking-human-intent-to-cad-parametric-cad-model-generation-via-cooperative-multi-task-alignment-and-spatial-aware-reinforcement-learning"></a>

### 5122\. [Rethinking Human Intent-to-CAD: Parametric CAD Model Generation via Cooperative Multi-Task Alignment and Spatial-Aware Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15681)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15681 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15681)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15681.pdf)]

**Authors:** Qingwang Zhang, Jiahao Li, Xiangdong Zhou

**Abstract:** Parametric Computer-Aided-Design (CAD) modeling from human intent remains challenging, particularly during the conceptual design stage, where design goals are expressed through incomplete and unstructured modalities (e.g., hand-drawn sketches and textual descriptions). In this work, we rethink the human intent-to-CAD pipeline and propose a unified method that directly maps multi-level human intents to executable codes, without assuming the prior existence of target CAD models. To support our study, we construct HiCAD, the first large-scale dataset aligning hand-drawn sketches, textual descriptions, and parametric CAD codes. Based on this, we introduce HiCAD, a two-stage framework comprising Cooperative Multi-Task Alignment to bridge the representational gap between heterogeneous inputs, and Spatial-Aware Reinforcement Learning to enforce geometric and topological consistency. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method significantly outperforms existing baselines across multiple tasks, validating its effectiveness and robustness in transforming heterogeneous human intents into high-fidelity parametric CAD models. Our project page: https://zqwlearning.github.io/HiCAD.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: HiCAD generates executable parametric 3D CAD models from sketches and text using cooperative multi-task alignment and spatial-aware reinforcement learning, outperforming existing baselines while enforcing geometric and topological consistency.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5123-unravel-recovering-an-interactive-3d-scene-from-a-single-image"></a>

### 5123\. [Unravel: Recovering an Interactive 3D Scene from a Single Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16146)
**ArXiv:** 2610.16146 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16146)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16146.pdf)]

**Authors:** Hui Shan, Keyang Luo, Binjia Zhou, ..., Zhen Chen, Xiangru Huang

**Abstract:** We present Unravel, a training-free framework that turns a single image into an interactive 3D scene, where every object is an independent and movable asset. Existing generators either produce one entangled geometry in which no object can be moved, or predict all objects in one feed-forward pass, where a wrong guess cannot be detected or repaired. Unravel instead takes a different approach: it first takes the scene apart, and then puts it back together. Starting from the input image, an agentic harness that iteratively peels the image layer by layer, and adopts point cloud as major modality for spatial relationship recovery. Specifically, a vision-language model plans which objects are fully visible, an image editor removes them and restores the occluded regions. Every edit is verified before being incorporated into the reconstruction. This process gradually turns occluded parts of the scene into direct observation, allowing objects to be reconstructed when they are best exposed rather than hallucinated from the original image alone. The scene is then rebuilt in reverse removal order, so that the supporting objects are naturally placed before the objects they support. Since a single-view image only reveals part of an object, we treat the observed points as a lower bound on its size: an asset may grow to cover its points, but can not shrink away from them. Finally, we physically settle the reconstructed scene to remove floating objects and interpenetrations, followed by an agentic audit that corrects remaining placement errors. Extensive experiments on diverse indoor scenes show that Unravel recovers more objects, places them more faithfully, and produces scenes that are more physically-plausible than existing methods.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Unravel generates an interactive 3D scene from a single image through VLM-guided object removal, generative restoration of occluded regions, and object-wise reconstruction, with experiments showing improved object recovery, placement fidelity, and physical plausibility.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5129-hairflow-modeling-3d-hair-strands-with-multi-modal-conditioned-diffusion"></a>

### 5129\. [HairFlow: Modeling 3D Hair Strands with Multi-Modal Conditioned Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17348)
**ArXiv:** 2610.17348 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17348)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17348.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yating Wang, Kui Wu, Ran Yi, ..., Lizhuang Ma, Yuefan Shen

**Abstract:** Modeling 3D hair strands is challenging due to complex hairstyle geometry, scarce high-quality training data, and incomplete or noisy input observations. Foundation models offer broader hairstyle priors, but their outputs are not directly usable for strand-based rendering and simulation. Existing approaches require costly per-instance optimization to fit hair priors to meshes from foundation models and 2D orientations from specialized predictors trained on limited hair data, restricting efficiency and generalization. We present HairFlow, a multimodal conditioned diffusion framework for efficient and controllable hair strands modeling. HairFlow conditions strand generation on two complementary representations: a coarse mesh that constrains global shape and multi-view sketches that specify local strand flow. HairFlow combines an Orientation VAE with a conditioned diffusion transformer to generate volumetric fields that are traced into explicit strands. To overcome the lack of paired sketch-strand training data, we introduce an automatic synthesis pipeline that generates geometrically aligned multi-view sketches with diverse styles representative of inference-time inputs. Experiments show that HairFlow generates plausible 3D strands that faithfully follow the input conditions across diverse hairstyles, substantially reduces inference time, and supports flexible multimodal control.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: HairFlow combines an Orientation VAE with a conditioned diffusion transformer to generate explicit 3D hair strands from coarse meshes and multi-view sketches, substantially reducing inference time while supporting controllable generation.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5144-sceneae-scene-generation-and-reconstruction-in-queryable-3d-anchored-latent-space"></a>

### 5144\. [SceneAE: Scene Generation and Reconstruction in Queryable 3D-Anchored Latent Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19708)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19708 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19708)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19708.pdf)]

**Authors:** Shuofeng Sun, Wenzhao Zheng, Yunpeng Zhang, ..., Linqing Zhao, Haibin Yan

**Abstract:** Recent video world models have increasingly adopted 3D memory mechanisms to integrate historical observations into a lightweight and persistent scene representation. This naturally raises a complementary question: if 3D representations can serve as an effective mechanism for memorizing observed scenes, can they also be directly used as the latent space for reconstructing and generating scenes? Motivated by this idea, we represent a scene as a compact and unstructured 3D-anchored latent space (\textbf{SceneAE}). Each latent combines an explicit 3D position with an implicit content feature and is spatially grounded to describe its local scene region, forming a sparse latent point cloud. Unlike conventional video-VAE latents that describe how a scene appears from particular viewpoints, our representation directly describes the scene itself, making it naturally applicable to both reconstruction and generation. For reconstruction, once the scene has been generated, our persistent 3D representation can be projected and decoded into RGB-D observations by different target cameras, without the need to regenerate a video for each new camera trajectory. For generation, we leverage the geometry-content separation to first generate camera-controlled 3D geometry and then synthesize content conditioned on it, reducing camera-appearance entanglement and improving camera controllability. Experiments show that this 3D latent achieves superior generation quality, computational cost, and camera controllability.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: introduces a compact 3D-anchored latent point cloud for scene generation, separating geometry generation from conditioned content synthesis and reporting improvements in generation quality, computational cost, and camera controllability.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5146-lifi-lidar-generation-from-multi-view-images-via-geometric-and-semantic-collaborative-guidance"></a>

### 5146\. [LiFi: LiDAR Generation from Multi-View Images via Geometric and Semantic Collaborative Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19786)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19786 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19786)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19786.pdf)]

**Authors:** Sizhuo Zhou, Xiaosong Jia, Fanrui Zhang, ..., Juyong Zhang, Junchi Yan

**Abstract:** Generating LiDAR point clouds from multi-view camera images is a valuable task with applications in controllable data synthesis, cross-modal simulation and various perception tasks. However, most existing methods are not specifically designed for this task, and the quality of their generated LiDAR remains limited. Moreover, these methods typically focus only on semantic cues in images or generate LiDAR from a monocular camera image. In this work, we introduce LiFi (LiDAR From images), a dedicated framework for generating visually and geometrically aligned LiDAR scenes from multi-view camera images. LiFi guides the latent diffusion process through two complementary branches: geometry and semantics. The geometry branch constructs scene features from images through a reverse sampling algorithm and an uncertainty-aware geometric encoder combined with DepthAnything3, whereas the semantic branch provides high-level semantic representations via a cross-view interaction module. We further introduce a dual-branch balanced classifier-free guidance (CFG) strategy, which enhances the model's conditional generation capability while preserving the independent learning and collaborative controllability of the two branches. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LiFi outperforms state-of-the-art methods in generating high-fidelity LiDAR scenes. The code will be publicly available at: https://github.com/szzhou88/LiFi.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: LiFi generates 3D LiDAR scenes from multi-view images through geometry- and semantics-guided latent diffusion with balanced dual-branch classifier-free guidance, outperforming state-of-the-art methods in generation fidelity.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5150-physforge-material-controllable-3d-reconstruction-with-separable-components"></a>

### 5150\. [PhysForge: Material-Controllable 3D Reconstruction with Separable Components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13681)
**ArXiv:** 2610.13681 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13681)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13681.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yuchen Wang, Yanqi Bao, Zheng Gu, ..., Jieqi Shi, Jing Huo

**Abstract:** Recent advances in physical 3D generation have enabled visually faithful assets with simulatable mechanical behavior. However, existing methods provide limited control over material behavior beyond appearance-based inference and struggle to estimate physical properties from complex interactions between objects and semantic parts. Therefore, we present PhysForge, a Video Diffusion Model (VDM)-based framework for addressing these challenges, comprising two complementary modules for material-controllable physical fitting and componentlevel interaction modeling. The first, PhysFit, leverages material-conditioned VDM dynamics as references for physical-property fitting, introducing windowed fitting to focus on material-revealing interactions and Hallucinated Forcing to disentangle nonphysical motion hallucinated by the VDM from material responses. The second, PhysComp decomposes complex interactions into individual objects and semantic components, enabling their physical parameters to be optimized independently and flexibly composed for joint simulation. Experiments on PhysAssets and Google Scanned Objects demonstrate that, compared with existing methods, PhysForge provides stronger control over material-dependent physical responses while supporting component-level physical modeling for complex interactions. It achieves the best CLIP and physical controllability scores on both common and uncommon materials, with qualitative comparisons, component-level ablations, and a user study further supporting these improvements. Our project page is available at https://masterwanna.github.io/PhysForge.html .

**Comment:** Criterion 5: PhysForge advances material-controllable physical 3D generation through material-conditioned video diffusion, windowed physical-property fitting, and component-level modeling, achieving the best CLIP and physical-controllability scores on PhysAssets and Google Scanned Objects.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5154-align-vae-aligning-attention-and-geometry-in-vecset-vaes-for-3d-reconstruction"></a>

### 5154\. [Align-VAE: Aligning Attention and Geometry in VecSet VAEs for 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14734)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14734 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14734)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14734.pdf)]

**Authors:** Huaizhi Qu, Mingxin Yang, Xintong Han, Fangneng Zhan, Chunchao Guo

**Abstract:** Set-based variational autoencoders (VecSet VAEs) represent 3D shapes as compact sets of latent tokens. Although each token originates from a query at a sampled surface location, whether these latents truly align with geometry remains unclear. We investigate this alignment and identify four issues: (i) attention-sink-like tokens that attract queries from across the shape; (ii) spatially periodic repetitions in decoder attention responses; (iii) mismatched normal information in encoder and decoder query features; and (iv) missing supervision of the intrinsic relation between signed distance fields and surface normals. To address these issues, we introduce Align-VAE with four corresponding modifications: (i) a shared bank of learnable decoder tokens provides a workspace for global computation; (ii) distance-biased cross-attention guides retrieval toward nearby predicted spatial anchors; (iii) normal-free encoder queries align the two stages' access to normal information while retaining normals in the encoder keys and values; and (iv) a screened Poisson-inspired regularizer supervises surface values and normal-direction derivatives. These changes align latent retrieval and field prediction with geometry without enlarging the per-shape code. On the ABO dataset, Align-VAE reduces Chamfer distance by 26.6% compared to the standard VecSet VAE.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 5: Align-VAE improves a generative 3D shape autoencoder through geometry-aligned latent retrieval and a screened Poisson-inspired regularizer, reducing Chamfer distance by 26.6% on ABO without enlarging the per-shape code.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5167-code2worlds-2-physically-plausible-agent-harness-for-embodied-world-simulation"></a>

### 5167\. [Code2Worlds-2: Physically Plausible Agent Harness for Embodied World Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17651)
**ArXiv:** 2610.17651 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17651)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17651.pdf)]

**Authors:** Haoyu Zhang, Siming Li, Zeyu Zhang, ..., Tat-Seng Chua, Hao Tang

**Abstract:** Recent coding large language models (LLMs) can synthesize dynamic 4D worlds from natural language, but the resulting content remains a visual artifact. It lacks physical semantics such as the collision geometry, mass, friction, inertia, and joint structure, required to enable it to simulate the physical world, and its dynamics are scripted to look plausible rather than designed to execute. What is needed is an executable world: one that a physics engine can load and step, resolving contact physically. We present Code2Worlds-2, an extension of Code2Worlds that turns 4D world generation into an embodied world simulation. We keep the original dual-stream generation pipeline intact and append two modules. First, a SimReady Scene Compiler that turns a generated visual scene into a simulator-compatible representation: it assigns collision meshes via convex decomposition, estimates physical attributes with a vision-language model, infers a lightweight support graph, instantiates the engine's contact, friction, and dynamics constraints, and applies a gravity settling pass, before exporting the scene as MuJoCo MJCF. Second, a Simulator-in-the-Loop 4D Refiner that implements an automated agent harness-a compile, simulate, critique and refine loop-that converts visual dynamics into executable rigid and articulated dynamics and evolves the physical settings rather than only the code, replacing the purely visual motion critic with a simulator critic that inspects penetration depth, collision pairs, stability, contact forces, joint limits, and robot reachability. We further propose SimCode4D, a state-based benchmark with five task templates for evaluating embodied simulation. Experiments on SimCode4D and across natural, indoor, industrial, urban, and service scenes show that Code2Worlds-2 produces physically plausible, simulator-compatible, and robot-interactable scenes.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Code2Worlds-2 improves the physical fidelity of LLM-generated 3D/4D worlds through a SimReady Scene Compiler and simulator-in-the-loop refinement, with evaluation on the SimCode4D benchmark.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5172-citycode-building-agent-operable-3d-cities-from-sparse-aerial-observations"></a>

### 5172\. [CityCode: Building Agent-Operable 3D Cities from Sparse Aerial Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18342)
**ArXiv:** 2610.18342 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18342)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18342.pdf)]

**Authors:** Jiahui Sun, Ran Bi, Yifei Liu, ..., Zhihang Zhong, Hongjie Zhang

**Abstract:** Urban spatial intelligence calls for 3D cities that agents can measure, interpret, and modify across successive tasks. Maintaining such cities as persistent, code-based states requires lightweight acquisition and support for consistent state updates without repeated dense reconstruction. We therefore formulate a new setting for grounded urban world construction: generating large-scale, instance-level parameterized 3D cities from one low-altitude aerial RGB image per region and known camera parameters, while recovering metrically scaled buildings and explicit road geometry and connectivity. We introduce CityCode, an agent-operable representation that unifies metric geometry, urban topology, visual semantics, and 3D assets through persistent entity identities, with dependency-aware versioned execution for validated updates and historical restoration. We further develop Air2Code to construct this representation by combining visual structure extraction with adapted metric depth estimation. To address occlusion and incomplete coverage, recovered structure and observed appearance guide generative completion and subsequent structure-aware 3D generation for buildings and backgrounds. Experiments on CitySample-Aerial and UrbanScene3D demonstrate substantially more accurate metric structure recovery and superior visual fidelity to the target cities compared with the evaluated baselines. Across 50 task chains of 50 steps each, CityCode supports continued querying, editing, planning, and historical restoration over evolving city states. Together, CityCode and Air2Code turn sparse aerial observations into persistent, agent-operable urban worlds for long-horizon urban spatial intelligence.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: Air2Code combines generative completion with structure-aware 3D generation to construct cities from one aerial RGB image per region, achieving superior visual fidelity on CitySample-Aerial and UrbanScene3D.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5177-magicgarment-benchmarking-single-image-standalone-3d-garment-reconstruction"></a>

### 5177\. [MagicGarment: Benchmarking Single-Image Standalone 3D Garment Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19623)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19623 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19623)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19623.pdf)]

**Authors:** Jia Chen, Ruyang Zhao, Jun Bai, ..., Saishang Zhong, Xinrong Hu

**Abstract:** Single-image 3D reconstruction has advanced rapidly, yet standalone garments remain challenging due to thin-shell geometry, open boundaries, and layered structures. Existing datasets and evaluation protocols provide limited support for systematic evaluation in this setting. We introduce MagicGarment, a benchmark for single-image standalone 3D garment reconstruction with 1,518 professionally constructed assets across 17 composite garment types, aligned multimodal annotations, and a unified evaluation protocol. We further establish a garment-aware reference baseline by adapting a pretrained image-to-3D backbone at two levels: Grounded Semantic Refinement (GSR) provides garment-focused visual conditioning, while Flow-Time-Route LoRA (FTR-LoRA) adapts the frozen 3D generative backbone using flow-timestep, token-routing, and expert-load information. Experiments demonstrate MagicGarment's benchmark utility and the effectiveness of garment-aware adaptation. Project page: https://1019538157.github.io/MagicGarment/.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: MagicGarment benchmarks single-image 3D garment reconstruction using 1,518 assets and introduces Grounded Semantic Refinement and Flow-Time-Route LoRA to adapt a pretrained image-to-3D generative backbone.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="5180-trajcfg-token-wise-classifier-free-guidance-via-denoising-trajectory-feedback"></a>

### 5180\. [TrajCFG: Token-Wise Classifier-Free Guidance via Denoising Trajectory Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14153)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14153 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14153)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14153.pdf)]

**Authors:** Jingyu Liu, Hao Jiang, Yikang Ding, ..., Pengfei Wan, Kun Gai

**Abstract:** Classifier-free guidance (CFG) typically applies a shared scale to all latent tokens, although their velocity predictions evolve differently during sampling. We introduce Trajectory-Adaptive Classifier-Free Guidance (TrajCFG), a training-free method for flow-matching models that adapts token-wise guidance using historical changes in guided velocity direction. TrajCFG maps this feedback to bounded scales, assigning stronger guidance to larger directional changes and weaker guidance to more consistent directions. It combines adjacent-step and interval-normalized longer-span feedback, reusing standard CFG predictions without additional model evaluations. Controlled diagnostics and ablations examine the roles of directional feedback, token-wise allocation, and history-span fusion. Experiments on text-to-video, image-to-video, text-to-audio-video, text-to-image, and image-to-3D generation show improvements over base samplers across multiple backbones. For text-to-video generation, TrajCFG improves VBench Total scores by 0.30-0.75 points over the strongest evaluated baseline on each of three backbones. These results support historical directional feedback as a broadly applicable signal for fine-grained guidance allocation. The project page is available at https://jyliu-98.github.io/trajcfg-project-page/.

**Comment:** Criterion 5: TrajCFG introduces training-free, token-wise guidance based on denoising-trajectory feedback and reports improved image-to-3D generation over base samplers without additional model evaluations.

**Relevance:** 7
Back to [[topic](#topic-5)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6005-compactsplat-spatially-adaptive-gaussian-distribution-for-feedforward-scene-reconstruction"></a>

### 6005\. [CompactSplat: Spatially Adaptive Gaussian Distribution for Feedforward Scene Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13177)
**ArXiv:** 2610.13177 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13177)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13177.pdf)]

**Authors:** Bing He, Jingnan Gao, Yunuo Chen, ..., Li Song, Wenjun Zhang

**Abstract:** Reconstructing 3D scenes from sparse views without per-scene optimization remains highly challenging, especially for recovering accurate geometry and fine textures. While recent feedforward approaches leverage generalizable 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) for scene generation, they typically assign one or multiple Gaussians to each pixel. Such uniform allocation not only produces highly redundant representations by wasting primitives in homogeneous areas, but also fails to exploit the inherent geometric priors of planar surfaces, often resulting in sub-optimal structural modeling. To address these limitations, we present CompactSplat, a novel feedforward framework that enables a content-aware allocation of 3D Gaussians. By integrating texture-guided spatial partitioning with hierarchical sampling, CompactSplat adaptively distributes primitives - concentrating dense Gaussians in complex regions while tiling flat areas with expanded, scale-modulated primitives. Moreover, our framework inherently supports dynamic sparsity control at inference time, enabling seamless trade-offs between rendering fidelity and memory footprint without requiring network retraining. Extensive experiments on RealEstate10K, DL3DV, and ScanNet demonstrate that CompactSplat consistently outperforms prior methods on both standard metrics and high-resolution rendering consistency, achieving high-fidelity reconstructions with significantly fewer primitives.

**Comment:** Matches criterion 6: CompactSplat introduces content-adaptive Gaussian allocation for feedforward sparse-view 3D reconstruction, outperforming prior methods on RealEstate10K, DL3DV, and ScanNet while using significantly fewer primitives.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6075-cotl-compact-token-learning-for-3d-scene-representation"></a>

### 6075\. [CoTL: Compact Token Learning for 3D Scene Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18446)
**ArXiv:** 2610.18446 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18446)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.18446.pdf)]

**Authors:** Han Liang, Sibo Wu, Anpei Chen, Rüdiger Westermann

**Abstract:** We present CoTL, a framework that learns a compact set of tokens that preserves scene geometry and appearance from unposed multi-view images. Our encoder compresses the input images into small per-view sets of latent tokens that aggregate information across views to form a view-consistent scene representation. Each additional view expands the representation with only a small, resolution-independent set of tokens, allowing its capacity to grow with the available observations. To force this compact bottleneck to retain sufficient scene information, a query-based Gaussian decoder uses scene-independent queries to attend to the learned tokens and recover an explicit, renderable set of 3D Gaussian primitives. Novel-view rendering supervision trains the compact representation end to end through the reconstructed Gaussian scene. Experiments on \retenk{} and ACID show that the resulting compact, unstructured token set supports competitive novel-view synthesis and can serve as the latent space of a generative model for 3D Gaussian scene generation. Together, these results show that 3D Gaussian reconstruction is an effective objective for compact scene representation learning and the learned token space provides a basis for broader downstream tasks in 3D vision.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: CoTL reconstructs renderable 3D Gaussian scenes from unposed multi-view images using compact per-view tokens and a query-based Gaussian decoder, achieving competitive novel-view synthesis while also supporting generative modeling in its learned latent space.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6095-holi-spatial-360-generative-reconstruction-from-sparse-panoramas-for-spatial-rendering-and-intelligence"></a>

### 6095\. [Holi-Spatial 360: Generative Reconstruction from Sparse Panoramas for Spatial Rendering and Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19502)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19502 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19502)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19502.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yuanyuan Gao, Zhirui Liu, Yuning Gong, ..., Dan Xu, Zhihang Zhong

**Abstract:** Spatial intelligence requires scalable 3D environments that are both photorealistic and semantically grounded, yet existing indoor datasets are dominated by room-scale captures and costly LiDAR acquisition. Online real-estate platforms offer abundant panoramas of diverse multi-room homes, but sparse viewpoints and texture-poor regions make reconstruction severely under-constrained. We introduce Holi-Spatial 360, a generative reconstruction and data curation framework for converting these panoramas into annotated multi-room 3D environments. Because reconstruction quality bounds subsequent generation, our Correspondence-Guided Geometric Optimization converts feed-forward reconstruction priors into reliable cross-view constraints and builds a coherent 3D Gaussian Splatting representation as 3D spatial memory. Adapted single-image and video priors then restore appearance and complete continuous trajectories while preserving observed content. The reconstructed scenes support arbitrary RGB-D rendering and an automated curation pipeline that combines end-to-end 3D grounding with VLM-based verification. Holi360 contains over 1K multi-room 3DGS scenes, more than 100K instance annotations, and 2M spatial question-answer pairs. We also introduce the Holi360 benchmark to evaluate the spatial understanding of VLMs in real-world, multi-room indoor environments. Experiments demonstrate superior reconstruction and curation quality under sparse panoramic inputs, while fine-tuning VLMs on Holi360 substantially improves spatial reasoning capabilities.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: Correspondence-Guided Geometric Optimization reconstructs coherent 3D Gaussian Splatting scenes from sparse panoramas, while adapted image and video generative priors restore appearance and complete continuous trajectories.

**Relevance:** 10
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6107-volume-rendering-with-metric-phase-fields-for-thin-structures-and-sign-ambiguous-geometry"></a>

### 6107\. [Volume Rendering with Metric--Phase Fields for Thin Structures and Sign-Ambiguous Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13088)
**ArXiv:** 2610.13088 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13088)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.13088.pdf)]

**Authors:** Junkai Deng, Jiayi Kong, Xuhui Chen, ..., Wenping Wang, Ying He

**Abstract:** Neural implicit surface methods that couple volume rendering with signed distance functions (SDFs), such as VolSDF and NeuS, achieve high-quality multi-view reconstruction for watertight objects. However, these approaches rely on a globally consistent inside-outside partition of space. This assumption becomes fragile for thin structures (e.g., sheets, tubes, and thin walls) and can break down for sign-ambiguous geometry (e.g., surfaces with boundary, non-manifold junctions, and closely spaced surface components), often resulting in spurious closures or loss of fine structures. Unsigned distance field (UDF) formulations relax the global sign requirement and better support non-watertight geometry, but are substantially harder to optimize. We propose PhaseSurf, a neural volume rendering framework built on Metric-Phase Fields (MPFs). MPFs decompose geometry into a nonnegative metric field capturing unsigned proximity and a continuous phase field providing a soft sign cue. By coupling the two through residual phase injection, MPFs yield a signed composite implicit function with stable near-surface gradients while retaining UDF-like robustness in sign-ambiguous regions. This design enables direct iso-surface extraction using standard Marching Cubes and avoids the failure modes of both pure SDF- and UDF-based formulations. We further introduce MPF-specific training losses that enforce metric consistency, stabilize phase transitions near the interface, and suppress spurious geometry in free space. Experiments on multi-view datasets with thin structures and sign-ambiguous geometry show that PhaseSurf produces more stable reconstructions and higher-quality geometry than UDF-based methods and recent 3DGS pipelines, while avoiding artifacts typical of SDF-based approaches.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: PhaseSurf introduces Metric–Phase Fields for neural volume-rendered multi-view reconstruction, enabling standard Marching Cubes extraction of thin and sign-ambiguous geometry while reporting more stable reconstructions and higher-quality geometry than UDF methods and recent 3DGS pipelines.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6116-pure-refining-feed-forward-3d-reconstruction-with-unlabeled-post-training"></a>

### 6116\. [PURE: Refining Feed-forward 3D Reconstruction with Unlabeled Post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14698)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14698 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14698)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14698.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yaolong Zhu, Peng Li, Jiahao Lu, ..., Ronggang Wang, Yuan Liu

**Abstract:** Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have achieved remarkable progress through large-scale training and strong learned priors. However, they remain fragile when applied to dynamic in-the-wild videos, where non-rigid motion, occlusions, and illumination changes are prevalent. A key limitation lies in their reliance on expensive depth and camera pose annotations, which hinders scaling to diverse real-world dynamic data and restricts generalization. To address this issue, we propose \textbf{PURE}, an unlabeled post-training refinement framework that improves depth and camera estimation without requiring ground-truth supervision. Instead of relying on annotations, PURE elegantly distills robust structural knowledge from state-of-the-art 2D priors into 4D feed-forward architectures. PURE leverages relative depth constraints, affine-invariant geometry distillation, and pose refinement based on feature correspondences to stabilize geometric predictions under challenging dynamic conditions. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world dynamic benchmarks demonstrate that PURE delivers consistent improvements across multiple state-of-the-art architectures. Furthermore, our data-efficient paradigm effectively mitigates domain shifts, successfully unlocking the architectural limits of these base models in challenging, in-the-wild dynamic scenes.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: PURE improves dynamic feed-forward 3D reconstruction through unlabeled post-training using affine-invariant geometry distillation and feature-correspondence-based camera-pose refinement, with consistent gains across multiple architectures on synthetic and real-world dynamic benchmarks.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6125-graph3r-unifying-view-graph-initialization-with-feed-forward-reconstruction"></a>

### 6125\. [Graph3R: Unifying View Graph Initialization with Feed-Forward Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16272)
**ArXiv:** 2610.16272 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16272)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.16272.pdf)]

**Authors:** Jingwen Yu, Hengli Wang, Jianhao Jiao, ..., Ping Tan, Hong Zhang

**Abstract:** Feed-forward reconstruction recovers camera poses and dense geometry from image sets whose views are assumed to come from a single scene. In practice, uncurated image collections often contain views from disjoint scenes, and reconstructing these views jointly can corrupt camera poses and geometry. Existing pipelines typically resolve view associations in separate stages before final reconstruction, leaving view graph initialization and reconstruction inference only loosely coupled. We introduce Graph3R, which unifies view graph initialization with feed-forward reconstruction through a shared geometric representation. A lightweight verification head predicts pairwise covisibility from the multi-view features of $π^3$ to construct a view graph. This graph classifies the input views into scene groups. For each group, we mask the shared features before decoding camera poses and dense geometry. Reusing the multi-view features allows Graph3R to reconstruct disjoint scenes separately with a single backbone pass. Across three benchmarks, Graph3R improves average maximum recall at 100% precision by upto 8.99 percentage points over the baseline, while improving reconstruction quality on mixed-scene inputs. Regarding the multi-session mapping task, Graph3R improves cross-session localization and achieves competitive pose estimation accuracy, while delivering a runtime speedup of approximately $35.9\times$ over the baseline when reconstructing the query group. By integrating learned view association with geometric inference, our approach takes a step toward end-to-end reconstruction from uncurated image collections.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: Graph3R jointly estimates camera poses and dense geometry using shared π³ features and learned view associations, enabling reconstruction of disjoint scenes in a single backbone pass and improving average maximum recall at 100% precision by up to 8.99 percentage points.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6131-physical-slam-physics-aware-simultaneous-localisation-and-mapping"></a>

### 6131\. [Physical SLAM: Physics-aware Simultaneous Localisation and Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17260)
**ArXiv:** 2610.17260 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17260)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17260.pdf)]

**Authors:** Guangming Wang, Zhishuo Liu, Haibing Wu, ..., Olaf Wysocki, Brian Sheil

**Abstract:** To reconstruct a predictive 4D representation of the physical world from monocular video captured by a moving camera, we jointly reconstruct dynamic Gaussian radiance fields and infer their underlying physical dynamics. This paper proposes \emph{Physical SLAM}, a physics-aware method for constructing 4D Gaussian fields of deformable objects and predicting their future state when subjected to external actions, given a sequence of RGB-D images. Our method separates the scene into a static background Gaussian field for camera localization and a dynamic object Gaussian field driven by a spring--mass physical model of elastic deformation, inertia, and damping. To integrate visual observations with physical dynamics, we introduce virtual observation forces that gradually pull simulated surface nodes toward observed depth points, avoiding abrupt replacement of simulated node positions. Meanwhile, the physical parameters of the spring--mass model are updated from simulation errors aggregated over the full reconstruction observation window, enabling the reconstructed dynamics to align with observations. To couple appearance, geometry, and physics, we further design a physics-aware Gaussian transformation and surface alignment strategy, where dynamic Gaussians are transformed along the deforming physical surface of the spring--mass model and constrained to remain aligned with the surface during rendering-based optimization. The resulting representation supports observed-frame rendering and action-conditioned simulation, extending SLAM maps to describe how deformable objects respond to manipulation. On the PhysTwin benchmark, our method improves reconstruction and prediction PSNR over PhysTwin by 10.4\% and 11.0\%, respectively. On the TUM RGB-D benchmark, it reduces average camera tracking error by 20.6\%, improves SSIM by 3.6\%, and reduces LPIPS by 8.3\% compared with 4DGS-SLAM.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: Physical SLAM jointly performs camera localization and deformable 4D Gaussian reconstruction with a spring–mass physics model, improving reconstruction and prediction PSNR over PhysTwin by 10.4% and 11.0%, respectively.

**Relevance:** 9
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6151-mvffn-multi-view-floor-plan-feed-forward-network-for-unposed-wide-baseline-panorama-layout-reconstruction"></a>

### 6151\. [MvFFN: Multi-view Floor plan Feed-forward Network for Unposed Wide-Baseline Panorama Layout Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14315)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14315 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14315)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14315.pdf)]

**Authors:** Yuguang Li, Yichuan Deng, Zixuan Liu, ..., Linda Shapiro, Alex Colburn

**Abstract:** Reconstructing accurate camera poses and floor-plan layouts from sparse, unposed, wide-baseline RGB panoramas remains a challenging open problem. Direct multi-room floor-plan prediction is especially difficult in this setting: pose, geometry, semantics, and global layout structure are tightly coupled, yet a complete floor-plan is hard to learn as a single global output. Our key insight is to keep the model's predictions strictly local. Multi-view Ordered Wall Instance Segmentation (MOIS) is designed around this concept. Each panorama column predicts only the wall it observes plus the next two adjacent walls in clockwise order. The global wall partition, room layouts, and shared coordinates emerge from deterministic aggregation of overlapping local predictions. Building on MOIS, we propose Multi-view floor-plan Feed-Forward Network (MvFFN), which jointly predicts coarse camera poses, dense global geometry, and per-wall semantic outputs. A downstream Layout Chaining (LC) post processing pipeline then assembles these predictions into multi-room floor-plans with shared global coordinates, consistent wall identities, and connected room structure. The dense MOIS predictions recover cross-view wall identity and local within-room connectivity more accurately than composed modular baselines, and the assembled layouts substantially outperform prior methods on wall and junction level accuracy. We also introduce the ZInD-CrossView dataset, which augments ZInD with globally unique wall instances and cross-view segmentation labels for this task.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: MvFFN jointly predicts camera poses and dense global geometry from sparse, unposed wide-baseline panoramas, using local wall predictions and Layout Chaining to substantially improve wall- and junction-level reconstruction accuracy.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6155-drive3r-block-wise-streaming-geometric-foundation-models-for-autonomous-driving"></a>

### 6155\. [Drive3R: Block-wise Streaming Geometric Foundation Models for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15112)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15112 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15112)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15112.pdf)]

**Authors:** Tianyi Yang, Haojun Yang, Jipeng Xiao, ..., Li Jiang, Zhipeng Zhang

**Abstract:** High-fidelity closed-loop simulation for autonomous driving increasingly relies on dense multi-view 3D reconstruction. While recent feed-forward geometric foundation models have revolutionized per-scene optimization, applying them to continuous, long-horizon driving sequences is computationally intractable due to GPU memory limits and the quadratic complexity of global attention. Existing scaling strategies are fundamentally limited, as causal streaming suffers from compounding scale drift and trajectory deviation, while rigid "chunk-then-align" heuristics fragment global context and produce trajectory discontinuities. To address these limitations, we introduce Drive3R, a block-wise streaming geometric foundation model. Its block-wise causal attention resolves the scalability bottleneck of fully bidirectional attention while avoiding the loss of local future context in purely causal streaming. Its confidence-aware re-anchoring further mitigates long-range drift by detecting unreliable streaming states, re-initializing the KV cache, and stitching refreshed segments through SE(3) alignment. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Drive3R establishes a new state-of-the-art on nuScenes, KITTI, and Waymo. Specifically, on nuScenes, it improves over the recent DVGT-2 baseline from 0.1806 to 0.1081 in AbsRel, from 0.6750 to 0.5344 in point-cloud Accuracy, and from 1.5812 to 0.9092 in ATE.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: Drive3R advances long-horizon multi-view 3D reconstruction through block-wise causal attention and confidence-aware SE(3) re-anchoring, improving nuScenes point-cloud Accuracy from 0.6750 to 0.5344 and trajectory ATE from 1.5812 to 0.9092.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6160-sharper-frames-worse-rooms-viewpoint-coverage-dominates-image-quality-in-feed-forward-3d-reconstruction"></a>

### 6160\. [Sharper Frames, Worse Rooms: Viewpoint Coverage Dominates Image Quality in Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15854)
**ArXiv:** 2610.15854 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15854)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.15854.pdf)]

**Authors:** Alibek Dadajonov, Hikmatillo Umarhojiyev

**Abstract:** Grounded spatial reasoning about indoor scenes rests on a geometric reconstruction that the multimodal system does not itself produce. Feed-forward multi-view reconstruction models operate under a fixed view budget, making frame selection a critical upstream decision. We show that under this setting the conventional preference for sharp frames can be actively harmful. Across nine handheld room scans, selecting the sharpest 16 of 200 candidate frames is the worst strategy we test, reaching 48\% room dimension error where uniform temporal sampling reaches 3\%. We trace this failure to a coupling between image quality and camera motion: sharp frames tend to occur during slow motion and therefore concentrate the selection in a narrow angular region, leaving $192^\circ$ of the room unobserved in the case we illustrate and over $300^\circ$ in the worst cells. A $2\times2$ factorial experiment that independently manipulates viewpoint spread and per-frame sharpness shows that spread has roughly three times the main effect of quality, and that blurry but well spread selections outperform sharp but clustered selections in 8 of 9 captures. The advantage also diminishes as the frame budget increases and angular coverage saturates, consistent with this mechanism. Finally, we ask whether the failure is detectable at inference: the reconstruction model's multi-view consistency score increases with reconstruction error, while a vision-language model given the same frames reports confidence unrelated to its own spatial estimation error. Under small view budgets, viewpoint coverage is a more important constraint than per-frame image quality, and neither uncertainty signal available at inference reveals this upstream failure.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: Analyzes sparse-view feed-forward 3D reconstruction under a 16-frame budget, showing across nine room scans that viewpoint coverage has roughly three times the effect of image sharpness on reconstruction accuracy.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6165-gaussian-splatting-for-real-time-scene-reconstruction-methods-systems-and-trade-offs"></a>

### 6165\. [Gaussian Splatting for Real-Time Scene Reconstruction: Methods, Systems, and Trade-offs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17513)
**ArXiv:** 2610.17513 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17513)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.17513.pdf)]

**Authors:** Ashim Dahal, Rabab Abdelfattah, Nick Rahimi

**Abstract:** 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) allows for high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis. Since its inception in 2023, its explicit and highly configurable scene representation has been extended toward sparse-view generation, dynamic reconstruction, efficiency and compression, streaming, and simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM). Although Gaussian Splatting can provide real-time rendering, rendering an existing representation is distinct from constructing, updating, or maintaining the scene itself. In this survey, we synthesize current literature, categorize them and provide a connection of current approaches and tools towards an end-to-end pipeline. Explicitly, we categorize GS scene reconstruction into five overlapping families: single-shot generation, dynamic representations, efficiency and compression, streaming and immersive systems, and Gaussian Splatting SLAM. We distinguish render-only throughput from task-specific scene generation, update, tracking, mapping, and optimization costs, and combine controlled sparse-view benchmarking with native evaluations to identify cross-family quality, latency, memory, and update-cost trade-offs.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: This 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstruction survey includes controlled sparse-view benchmarking and compares quality, latency, memory, and scene-update costs across reconstruction approaches.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6176-remember-revisit-revise-scaffold-memory-for-long-horizon-3d-geometry"></a>

### 6176\. [Remember, Revisit, Revise: Scaffold Memory for Long-Horizon 3D Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19674)
**ArXiv:** 2610.19674 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19674)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.19674.pdf)]

**Authors:** Soumick Sarker, Mengmi Zhang

**Abstract:** Geometry foundation models estimate camera poses and 3D scene structure from video sequences, but maintaining consistent reconstructions over long horizons remains challenging. Feed-forward models process long sequences as independent, fixed-length frame windows and align their predictions, causing pose and reconstruction errors to accumulate across windows. Streaming models retain information from past frames to inform future predictions, but cannot revise earlier estimates when later observations reveal errors. To address this, we introduce Scaffold MemOry for Revision (SMORE), a framework equipping frozen geometry foundation models with a revisable external memory for long-horizon inference. Inspired by hippocampal associative memory in biological brains, which links spatial context with sensory experience, SMORE associates where an observer is with what it observes, enabling past observations to be stored, retrieved, verified against new evidence, and revised when inconsistencies are detected. We evaluate SMORE with eight competitive geometry models on five datasets spanning camera pose estimation, multi-view depth estimation, 3D point-cloud reconstruction, simultaneous localization and mapping (SLAM), and novel view synthesis. Across all evaluated models, SMORE consistently improves long-sequence pose estimation, reduces multi-view depth error by up to 58%, reduces Chamfer distance for 3D point-cloud reconstruction by up to 43%, and achieves average trajectory error as low as 2.9 cm in long-horizon SLAM. By retaining corrected poses and observations, SMORE further improves the fidelity and spatial consistency of large-scale 3D scene reconstruction. These results establish revisable external memory as a flexible interface for extending pretrained geometry models beyond fixed-context prediction toward iterative, error-correcting inference over long visual sequences.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: SMORE equips geometry foundation models with revisable external memory for camera-pose estimation and long-horizon 3D reconstruction, reducing multi-view depth error by up to 58% and point-cloud Chamfer distance by up to 43%.

**Relevance:** 8
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]

<a id="6181-ad-gs-anatomical-density-guided-3d-gaussian-splatting-for-sparse-view-dental-cbct-reconstruction"></a>

### 6181\. [AD-GS: Anatomical Density-guided 3D Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Dental CBCT Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14963)
**ArXiv:** 2610.14963 [[page](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14963)] [[pdf](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.14963.pdf)]

**Authors:** Zonglin Li, Kexian Xie, Feiran Li, ..., Zhanyu Ma, Heng Guo

**Abstract:** Sparse-view Cone-Beam Computed Tomography (CBCT) reconstruction is essential for reducing radiation dose and scan effort, but remains underconstrained due to limited projection information. Recent 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS)-based CBCT reconstruction methods rely on gradient-driven densification, which becomes unstable under sparse acquisition and often leads to over-smoothed structures. We propose AD-GS, an Anatomical Density-guided 3D Gaussian Splatting framework that formulates Gaussian densification as an attenuation-aware Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling process. By interpreting Gaussians as samples from an underlying anatomical attenuation distribution, AD-GS adaptively concentrates representation capacity in high-attenuation regions, enabling sharper structural boundaries under sparse projections. To further constrain reconstruction, we incorporate a semantic regularization based on zero-shot features from Segment Anything in Medical Images, enforcing multi-view consistency through semantic priors. Experiments on the ToothFairy3 dental CBCT dataset demonstrate that AD-GS achieves superior structural fidelity and boundary preservation compared with existing 3DGS-based approaches under sparse-view settings within the dental and maxillofacial setting. Code is available at https://github.com/yulinjiaxue/AD-GS-BMVC2026.

**Comment:** Criterion 6: AD-GS introduces attenuation-aware MCMC Gaussian densification for sparse-view 3D CBCT reconstruction, improving structural fidelity and boundary preservation on the ToothFairy3 dental dataset.

**Relevance:** 7
Back to [[topic](#topic-6)] [[top](#topics)]
